Let's design robust multi-strategy:
1. Generate q sequences for many n (e.g., 50, 100, 256, 512, 1024, 2048, 4096, 8192) using central binomial q_i = binom(2i,i)/4^i (can compute recurrence q_{i+1}=q_i*(2i+1)/(2i+2)). Normalize. Compute fast objective. This will provide baseline ~1.57 for large, worse for small.

But cap-1 coordinate maximization simple and could escape if we first shrink q slightly to create slack (scale factor η<1), then coordinate max may increase some coordinates beyond original, maybe net sum improvement. Algorithm:
- For a candidate n, we want maximize S such that b≤1. Starting from any nonnegative a (sum maybe arbitrary), scale to cap: if max_b>0, a = a / sqrt(max_b) so cap=1 (unless max=0). Then run coordinate boost (best response) to increase S while keeping b≤1.
After coordinate passes, we get feasible cap1 with some S. Objective =2n/S^2. Keep best.

Coordinate best response from random starts:
Function cap1_coordinate_improve(a, n, max_passes=20, eps=1e-12):
- Ensure a nonnegative, if max_b>0 scale to cap1: a = a / sqrt(max_b)
- b = conv(a,a) (FFT or direct) capped with tolerance.
- sum_prev maybe.
- For pass in range: changed=False; improve_sum=0; for i in random order maybe to avoid bias:
  x=a[i]
  # off-diag
  if n>1:
    j = np.arange(n)
    mask = j != i
    # Use arrays without allocation? lags = i + j[mask]
    aj = a[j[mask]]
    valid = aj > 1e-15
    if any valid:
       ratios = (cap - b[i + j[mask][valid]]) / (2*aj[valid])
       off_min = x + np.min(ratios)
    else off_min = cap_sqrt? (if all others zero, only diagonal)
  else off_min=cap_sqrt
  diag if 2i < len b:
     c_diag = cap - (b[2i] - x*x)
     if c_diag <0 due numerical set 0
     diag_min = sqrt(c_diag)
  new_x = min(off_min, diag_min, sqrt(cap)) # x maybe
  if new_x < x: new_x=x
  if new_x > x*(1+1e-12)+1e-12: update.
  d = new_x - x
  if d>0:
     # update a[i], b
     a[i]=new_x
     # update b: for k=i..i+n-1: if k==2i: add d*d + 2*x*d; else add 2*d*a_j where j=k-i (note if j updated? use new a? For j != i, a_j current (including previous coordinates) is correct. For j=i diagonal, using old x.)
     # vectorized: j = arange(n); if d !=0:
        # off: idx = i + j; off mask j != i; b[off_idx] += 2*d*a[off_j]
        # diag if 2i: b[2i] += d*d + 2*x*d
  After pass, if sum increase small stop.
Return a,b,sum.

Coordinate method exactness: For linear convolution, b_k=∑_{j=0}^{k} a_j a_{k-j} with range. For coordinate i, affected lags k=i+j (j=0..n-1). For j≠i, current contribution 2 a_i a_j. For j=i, contribution a_i^2. Other contributions independent. Yes.
Upper for j≠i: c = b_{i+j} - 2 a_i a_j. Need c≥0 (yes nonnegative). New x: c + 2 x a_j ≤1. If a_j>0: x ≤ (1-c)/(2a_j) = a_i + (1-b_{i+j})/(2a_j). Diag: c=b_{2i}-a_i^2. x ≤ sqrt(1-c). Good.
When there are zero a_j, no linear constraint; but if x increases, b_{i+j}=c unchanged, so no issue. Off diagonal min can be over positive a_j. If all a_j zero, only diag.
This coordinate update yields maximum feasible x given others fixed, because constraints are independent and each is interval [0, upper]. So exact.

This is a form of “constraint programming”. Starting from zero, if we repeatedly set coordinates to max, will it converge? Yes monotone. Starting from q scaled, it may increase.

Can we speed up by doing “coordinate scan” with random order and using previous updates. For cap1, after a coordinate increase, some slacks decrease; later coordinates' upper min will account. This is like Dykstra? Good.

Potential problem: If a_j is extremely small, ratio huge; ignore if a_j<tiny to avoid overflow, but constraint mostly unconstrained. However if a_j very small, increasing x may still matter; if ratio huge due small a, no issue. Use threshold maybe 1e-12.

Stop criteria: After full pass, sum increase < tol*sum or max change small. But with many starts, we may want limited passes.

Let's design cap1_coordinate_boost optimized:
```
def boost_cap1(a, n, max_passes=30, tol=1e-7, safety=1.0, rng=None, verbose=False):
    a = np.asarray(a, float).copy()
    a = np.clip(a,0,1000)
    n = a.size
    # initial scale to cap1 using fast_conv
    maxb = fast_max_conv(a)
    if maxb <= 0: return a...
    a *= 1.0 / np.sqrt(maxb * (1+1e-10))  # feasible
    b = fast_conv(a) # maybe recompute
    # clip tiny neg
    sum_a = a.sum()
    old_sum = 0
    for pass in range(max_passes):
        if old_sum > 0 and (sum_a - old_sum) <= tol * max(1,sum_a): break
        old_sum = sum_a
        # process in random or sequential order? sequential from 0 to n-1 may favor early; random may mix. Use fixed order maybe i from n-1 down to 0 to increase tail? Let's use multiple orders: reverse, random.
        for i in order:
            x = a[i]
            # diag upper
            diag_k = i+i
            if diag_k < 2*n-1:
                # c = b[diag_k] - x*x (should not include linear with i)
                val = 1.0 - (b[diag_k] - x*x)
                if val < 0: val = 0.0
                upper = np.sqrt(val)
            else:
                upper = np.inf
            # off diag
            # Need lags i+0..i+n-1, j=0..n-1, skip j=i
            # Could use arrays for all j; mask
            # For speed, precompute idx? arange each time expensive but okay.
            j = rng? no
            aj = a  # need subset
            # Use numpy with j index: lags = i + np.arange(n)
            # We can avoid allocation for j by two slices? lags are contiguous i..i+n-1. a_j = a[0..n-1] as whole; need skip i.
            slack_lags = 1.0 - b[i:i+n]  # length n
            # aj = a (length n), but off ratio uses a[j]; we need skip j=i.
            # We can compute ratios for all j with a>0, then set ratio[i]=inf
            pos = a > 1e-14
            ratios = np.full(n, np.inf)
            # To avoid division by zeros:
            ratios[pos] = slack_lags[pos] / (2.0 * a[pos])  ?? Wait slack_lags indexed by j, a[pos] same length. Yes lags i+j correspond to a[j].
            ratios[i] = np.inf
            # Also if slack negative set ratio -inf? For feasibility slack>=-tol; max(0,slack)
            off_min = x + ratios[np.isfinite(ratios)].min() if any
            new_x = min(upper, off_min, 1.0) # cap sqrt upper for x? sqrt(1)
            if new_x < x: new_x=x
            if new_x > x:
                d = new_x - x
                # update b impacted: lags i..i+n-1
                # off: 2d*a[j] for j != i
                # diag: d^2 + 2xd if j=i
                # Update vectorized:
                delta_lags = 2.0*d*a  # length n
                if 2*i < len(b):
                    # diag correction: delta_lags[i] currently 2d*a[i]=2xd; need d^2 as well
                    delta_lags[i] += d*d
                    # For j=i, lags index i+i, update with delta_lags[i]
                b[i:i+n] += delta_lags
                a[i]=new_x
                sum_a += d
        # feasibility correction:
        maxb = max(0, np.max(b))
        if maxb > 1.0 + 1e-9:
            a *= np.sqrt(1.0/maxb)
            b *= 1.0/maxb
            sum_a = a.sum()
    return a
```

Better use slack = 1-b; set negative to 0. Then increase to exactly cap for that active; if due numerical negative, set 0, no over. This is safe (slightly conservative). But if slack negative due real violation from previous, scaling after pass fixes.

    ratios = np.where((a > tiny) & (np.arange(n) != i), (1.0 - b[i:i+n]) / (2.0*a), np.inf)

```

Pseudo:
```

if i > 0:
    la = a[:i]; ls = np.clip(1.0 - b[i:2*i], 0, None)
    pos = la > tiny
    if np.any(pos): off_min = min(off_min, x + np.min(ls[pos]/(2*la[pos])))

a = np.clip(a,0,1000)
if sum<=0: return zeros

if maxb <= 0: ...

```
But for coordinate boost, we want cap slightly below1 to have slack? If start exactly cap (q), coordinate won't increase. So create scaled starts: `a = scale_to_cap1(base) * s` for s in (0.5,0.7,0.8,0.9). This gives max_b=s^2. coordinate can fill.

Base generators for length n:
- q: recurrence.
- reversed q
- scaled q + noise: `s*q + alpha*rng.random(n)` maybe with alpha 0-0.1.
- exponential increasing: 1+ i^p etc.
- power law with shift: (i+eps)^-alpha; for alpha 0.2,0.4,0.49,0.5.
- mixed: q_i^beta? beta 0.5,1,2.
- sinusoidal skew: 1+0.5*sin(π i/(n)) etc.
- block: uniform first half, etc.
- random exponential (rng.exponential).
- two-q mixture: s*q + t*rev_q with random s,t. This might intentionally add right tail.
- q shifted: q[i+k] maybe.
- random smooth: low-pass noise.
We can generate maybe 20 comfortable starts per n.

After coordinate boost, we can also run a “polish” using several orders. We can keep best cap1 a.

Additionally, we can do a local search on the parameter s for q+tail using coordinate boost? Not necessary if random starts.

FFT conv function:
```
def fast_conv(a):
    n = len(a)
    if n <= 256: return np.convolve(a,a, mode='full')
    nfft = 1
    target = 2*n-1
    while nfft < target: nfft <<=1
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, n=nfft)
    b = np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, n=nfft)[:target]
    # small negative
    b[b < 0] = 0 # Maybe overclip? small
    return b
```
For cap1 coordinate, if we clip negatives, sum b may slightly off. Good.
For n up to maybe 8192, nfft 16384, FFT fast.

But initial scale using fast_conv; if n small, direct exact. For n≤512, direct convolve O(n^2) C maybe okay. np.convolve for n=512 length 1023, O262k, fine. For 2048, O4M, maybe 0.01s? Actually C loop 4M, fine. We can just use np.convolve for all n≤2048 to be exact; for larger FFT. Let's set threshold 2048? np.convolve 2048 length gives 4M multiplies, C double, maybe 0.01-0.1s, okay. 4096 16M, maybe 0.1-0.5s. We can use direct for ≤1024, FFT for >1024.   if time.monotonic() > deadline: break
  if old>=0 and sum_a-old < 1e-7*max(1,sum_a): break
  old=sum_a
  if order==0: loop = range(n)
  elif order==1: range(n-1,-1,-1)
  else: loop = rng.permutation(n)
  for i in loop:
    # if time deadline break? check every 256 updates
    ... update
  # feasibility normalization
  maxb=b.max()
  if maxb > 1.0 + 1e-8:
     factor = np.sqrt(1.0/maxb)*0.999999
     a *= factor; b *= factor*factor; sum_a=a.sum()
return a,sum_a,b
```

Top-level `objective_z(n, z)`:
```
z=np.asarray(z)
a=np.exp(z - z.max())
a/=a.sum()
b=conv(a)
return 2*n*np.max(b)
```
Bounded simplex via softmax; no nonneg issue. Use `scipy.optimize.minimize(method='Nelder-Mead')` or Powell with multistarts. for n up to 12. Let's define `run_cap1_search(n, num_starts, max_passes, deadline, rng, order_mixture)`:

Simpler `improve_candidate` runs sequential orders on same a; but order1 may alter a before order2, not same as starting. We can in search do:
```
base=...
for s in slacks:
  a0=base*s scale? base already cap? We can base portion 
  for order in orders:
    a=a0.copy()
    a=boost(a, order, passes)
    update
```
This explores order basins.

0: q

5: q + noise
6: power law with random alpha/eps (decreasing) (x+eps)^-alpha

8: mixture q + c q_rev
9: mixture q + c rev_q + random

Need implement softmax objective efficiently for small n. Since n small, use direct convolution `np.convolve`. Objective: `2*n * max(conv) / sum^2` or softmax sum=1 => `2*n*max(conv)`. Parameter z length n-1 to avoid scale: a_last=1? Or use softmax over n variables; scale invariance may make contour flat; but Nelder-Mead okay. Better fix sum=1 by a = exp(z)/sum. To remove constant mode, set z[n-1]=0: first n-1 variables. Function:
```
def softmax_obj(params, n):
    z = np.empty(n); z[:n-1]=params; z[n-1]=0
    z -= z.max()
    a = np.exp(z); a /= a.sum()
    b = np.convolve(a,a)
    return 2*n*np.max(b)
```
Nonconvex, but for n≤12 okay. Use scipy.optimize.minimize(method='Nelder-Mead', options maxiter 10000). Multistart random normal. Also start from q log params (log(q_i/q_{n-1})). And random uniform.

Population size 20, maxiter 200; might be too slow but n small. We can implement own random hill climbing? Simpler: Use `scipy.optimize.minimize` with Powell, which is derivative-free and works multivariate. But local. Multistart enough.

Helper `q_sequence(n)`.

Helper `convolve_fast(a)`:
```
def convolve_fast(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float)
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return np.array([])
    if n <= 1024:
        return np.convolve(a,a)
    target=2*n-1
    nfft=1
    while nfft<target: nfft <<=1
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,n=nfft)
    out=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,n=nfft)[:target]
    # remove small negative
    out[out<0]=0
    return out
```
For scoring exact with evaluate_sequence, no need.

Helper `score_fast(a)`:
```
s=a.sum(); if s<1e-300 return inf
b=convolve_fast(a)
m=np.max(b)
return float(2*a.size*m/(s*s))
```
For a all zeros, inf.

Helper `score_exact(seq)`: call evaluate_sequence(seq). Since evaluate_security converts to float and clips; for our normals okay. Returns np.inf if invalid. Use try/except maybe.

Helper `normalize_seq(a)`:

if s<=0 return a
return a/s
```

Pseudo top-level:
```
def boost_cap1(a, order, max_passes, deadline, rng):
    a=np.array(a,float).copy()
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return a
    b=convolve_fast(a)
    maxb=np.max(b) if b.size else 0
    if maxb>0:
        a *= np.sqrt(1.0/maxb) * (1.0 - 1e-10)
        b *= (1.0 - 1e-10)**2 * (1.0/maxb) ? Actually if scale factor f=1/sqrt(maxb)*(1-1e-9), a*=f; b*=f*f.
    # if maxb==0, a zeros return
    sum_a=float(a.sum())
    old_sum=-1.0
    for _ in range(max_passes):
       if time.monotonic() > deadline - 2: break
       if old_sum >=0 and sum_a - old_sum < 1e-8*max(1.0,sum_a): break
       old_sum=sum_a
       if order==0:
          idx=np.arange(n)
       elif order==1:
          idx=np.arange(n-1,-1,-1)
       else:
          idx=rng.permutation(n)
       count=0
       for i in idx:
          count+=1
          if count & 63 ==0 and time.monotonic()>deadline-2: break
          x=float(a[i])
          # diag
          dl=2*i
          c = b[dl] - x*x
          val = 1.0 - c
          if val < 1e-12: val=0.0
          elif val > 1.0: val=1.0
          upper=np.sqrt(val)
          off_min=np.inf
          if i>0:
             la=a[:i]
             if np.any(la > 1e-14):
                ls=1.0 - a? need b[i:2*i]
                ls=np.clip(1.0 - b[i:2*i], 0.0, None)
                mask=la > 1e-14
                if mask.any():
                    ratio=np.min(ls[mask]/(2.0*la[mask]))
                    off_min=min(off_min, x+ratio)
          if i<n-1:
             ra=a[i+1:]
             if np.any(ra>1e-14):
                rs=np.clip(1.0 - b[2*i+1:i+n], 0.0, None)
                mask=ra>1e-14
                if mask.any():
                    ratio=np.min(rs[mask]/(2.0*ra[mask]))
                    off_min=min(off_min, x+ratio)
          new_x=upper
          if off_min < new_x: new_x=off_min
          if new_x < x: new_x=x
          if new_x > x:
             d=new_x - x
             # update b
             # if d not too small
             if d > 1e-15:
                delta = 2.0*d*a  # full a before updating i
                if dl < b.size:
                   delta[i] += d*d
                   b[i:i+n] += delta
                   # b[dl] includes delta[i]
                a[i]=new_x
                sum_a += d
             else:
                a[i]=new_x
       # after pass correct
       maxb=float(np.max(b)) if b.size else 0.0
       if maxb > 1.0 + 1e-8:
          f=np.sqrt(1.0/maxb)*(1.0-1e-9)
          a*=f; b*=f*f; sum_a=float(a.sum())
       elif maxb < 0.0: ...
    # final scale to cap1? For scoring, we can scale to maxb=1 to maximize? Scaling doesn't change score, but if maxb<1 due safety, score with maxb<1 equals same if using actual; if we scale to 1, score same. We'll return a.
    return a
```
But update `b[i:i+n] += delta` uses delta full a including a[i] old x. For off j=i, delta[i]=2dx + d^2. For j other, correct. However `b[i:i+n]` length n; what about lags affected by coordinate i when k=i+j for j=0..n-1, exactly. Good.
When updating a[i]=new_x after b update, delta used old a; fine.
Potential overflow if off_min huge? upper≤1, so new≤1. Values cap1≤1.
If n=1: i=0, off none, diag val=1-b0+x^2; if b0=x^2, upper=1. It will set a[0]=1. Good.

After boost, compute score:
```
def cap1_candidate_score(a):
   b=convolve_fast(a)
   m=np.max(b)
   s=a.sum()
   if m<=0 or s<=0: return inf
   return 2*a.size*m/(s*s)
```
Also we can rescale candidate to normalized or cap1. Return a as is.

Base generator top-level:
```
def generate_base(n, rng, kind):
    q = make_q(n)
    if kind==0: return q.copy()
    if kind==1: return q[::-1].copy()
    if kind==2: return rng.random(n)
    if kind==3: return rng.exponential(1.0,n)
    if kind==4: return q*np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.3,n))
    if kind==5: return q + 0.2*rng.random(n)
    kind choices random alpha:
    x=np.arange(n)
    alpha = rng.choice([0.3,0.4,0.49,0.5])
    eps = rng.choice([0.01,0.1,1.0])
    return (x+eps)**(-alpha)
    increasing: (x+1)**rng.choice([0.2,0.5,1.0])
    mixture: q*c1 + q_rev*c2
```
We can write a function with rng and kind int, using `math`/`np`.

def scale_to_cap_slack(a, s):

    m=convolve_fast_max(a) # maybe just max conv; full conv needed for score? Use convolve_fast
    if m<=0: return zeros
    a = a / np.sqrt(m)
    if s<1: a *= s
    return a
```

def run_softmax_search(n, rng, max_starts, deadline):
    best_x = np.log(q_sequence(n)) - np.log(q_sequence(n)[-1])  # But q last positive; x length n-1.
    best_f = softmax_objective(best_x, n)
    # maybe perturb best_x
    starts = []
    for _ in range(max_starts):
       if time > deadline: break
       if _==0: x0=best_x
       elif _==1: x0=rng.normal(0,1,n-1)
       elif _==2: x0=best_x + rng.normal(0,0.3,n-1)
       else: x0=rng.normal(0, rng.choice([0.5,1.0,2.0]), n-1)
       try res=sp_minimize(softmax_objective, x0, args=(n,), method='Nelder-Mead', options={'maxiter':5000, 'xatol':1e-7,'fatol':1e-10})
       except: pass
       f=res.fun
       if finite and f<best_f: best_x=res.x; best_f=f
    return best_f, best_x
```

```python
import numpy as np
import time

try:
    from scipy.optimize import minimize as sp_minimize
    from scipy.optimize import differential_evolution as sp_de
    HAVE_SCIPY = True
except Exception:
    HAVE_SCIPY = False


def make_q(n):
    """First n coefficients of (1-x)^(-1/2)."""
    q = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    if n <= 0:
        return q
    q[0] = 1.0
    for i in range(1, n):
        q[i] = q[i - 1] * ((2.0 * i - 1.0) / (2.0 * i))
    return q


def convolve_fast(a):
    """Full linear convolution, exact for modest n and FFT for larger n."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    if n <= 1024:
        return np.convolve(a, a, mode="full")
    target = 2 * n - 1
    nfft = 1
    while nfft < target:
        nfft <<= 1
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, n=nfft)
    out = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, n=nfft)[:target]
    # FFT roundoff can produce tiny negative values.
    out[np.abs(out) < 1e-12] = 0.0
    out[out < 0.0] = 0.0
    return out


def score_fast(a):
    """Fast proxy for evaluate_sequence."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return float("inf")
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return float("inf")
    b = convolve_fast(a)
    m = float(np.max(b)) if b.size else 0.0
    if not np.isfinite(m) or m <= 0.0:
        return float("inf")
    return float(2.0 * n * m / (s * s))


def softmax_objective(x, n):
    """Objective for softmax parameters x (length n-1), last log-weight fixed to 0."""
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    if x.size != n - 1:
        return float("inf")
    z = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    z[:-1] = np.clip(x, -12.0, 12.0)
    z[-1] = 0.0
    z -= np.max(z)
    w = np.exp(z)
    sw = float(np.sum(w))
    if sw <= 0.0:
        return float("inf")
    a = w / sw
    b = np.convolve(a, a, mode="full")
    return float(2.0 * int(n) * np.max(b))


def normalize_softmax(x, n):
    """Convert softmax parameters to a normalized nonnegative sequence."""
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    if x.size != n - 1:
        x = np.zeros(n - 1, dtype=np.float64)
    z = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    z[:-1] = np.clip(x, -12.0, 12.0)
    z[-1] = 0.0
    z -= np.max(z)
    w = np.exp(z)
    sw = float(np.sum(w))
    if sw <= 0.0:
        return np.full(n, 1.0 / n, dtype=np.float64)
    return w / sw


def run_softmax_search(n, rng, max_starts, deadline):
    """Derivative-free multistart local search on the simplex for small n."""
    q = make_q(n)
    if q.size != n:
        return None, float("inf")
    best_x = np.log(q[:-1] / max(q[-1], 1e-300)) if n > 1 else np.zeros(0)
    best_f = softmax_objective(best_x, n)

    if not HAVE_SCIPY:
        for _ in range(max(1, max_starts)):
            if time.monotonic() > deadline:
                break
            x = best_x + rng.normal(0.0, 0.5, size=n - 1)
            f = softmax_objective(x, n)
            if np.isfinite(f) and f < best_f:
                best_f = f
                best_x = x.copy()
        return best_x, best_f

    for k in range(max_starts):
        if time.monotonic() > deadline:
            break
        if k == 0:
            x0 = best_x.copy()
        elif k == 1:
            x0 = np.zeros(n - 1)
        elif k == 2:
            x0 = best_x + rng.normal(0.0, 0.25, size=n - 1)
        else:
            scale = float(rng.choice(np.array([0.3, 0.6, 1.0, 1.8])))
            x0 = rng.normal(0.0, scale, size=n - 1)

        try:
            res = sp_minimize(
                softmax_objective,
                x0,
                args=(n,),
                method="Nelder-Mead",
                options={"maxiter": 8000, "xatol": 1e-8, "fatol": 1e-12},
            )
            if np.isfinite(res.fun) and res.fun < best_f:
                best_f = float(res.fun)
                best_x = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64).copy()
        except Exception:
            pass

    # One more tailored Powell run from the best point.
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 5:
        try:
            res = sp_minimize(
                softmax_objective,
                best_x,
                args=(n,),
                method="Powell",
                options={"maxiter": 3000, "xtol": 1e-8, "ftol": 1e-12},
            )
            if np.isfinite(res.fun) and res.fun < best_f:
                best_f = float(res.fun)
                best_x = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64).copy()
        except Exception:
            pass

    return best_x, best_f


def make_random_base(n, kind, rng):
    """Diverse nonnegative starting vectors."""
    kind = int(kind) % 10
    q = make_q(n)
    qr = q[::-1].copy()
    x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)

    if kind == 0:
        return q.copy()
    if kind == 1:
        return qr.copy()
    if kind == 2:
        return rng.random(n, dtype=np.float64)
    if kind == 3:
        return rng.exponential(1.0, size=n, dtype=np.float64)
    if kind == 4:
        return q * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.35, size=n))
    if kind == 5:
        return q + 0.25 * rng.random(n, dtype=np.float64)
    if kind == 6:
        c1 = float(rng.uniform(0.2, 1.0))
        c2 = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 1.0))
        return c1 * q + c2 * qr
    if kind == 7:
        alpha = float(rng.choice(np.array([0.25, 0.35, 0.45, 0.49])))
        eps = float(rng.choice(np.array([0.05, 0.25, 1.0])))
        return (x + eps) ** (-alpha)
    if kind == 8:
        alpha = float(rng.choice(np.array([0.2, 0.5, 1.0, 1.6])))
        return (x / max(1, n - 1) + 0.05) ** alpha
    # kind 9: smooth skew/unimodal shape
    c = float(rng.uniform(-0.6, 0.6))
    d = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 0.8))
    y = 1.0 + c * np.sin(np.pi * x / max(1.0, float(n))) + d * (x / max(1.0, float(n)))
    return np.clip(y, 0.0, None)


def scale_base_to_slack(base, slack):
    """Scale a base vector to have convolution max about slack^2."""
    a = np.asarray(base, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, None)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if s <= 0.0:
        return np.zeros_like(a)
    b = convolve_fast(a)
    m = float(np.max(b)) if b.size else 0.0
    if not np.isfinite(m) or m <= 0.0:
        return np.zeros_like(a)
    a *= 1.0 / np.sqrt(m)
    a *= float(slack)
    return a


def boost_cap1(a, order, max_passes, deadline, rng):
    """
    Monotone coordinate best-response for the problem:
        maximize sum(a) subject to a >= 0 and conv(a,a) <= 1.
    The ratio improvement appears as increasing sum under the cap.
    """
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return a
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, None)
    if float(np.sum(a)) <= 0.0:
        return a

    b = convolve_fast(a)
    m = float(np.max(b)) if b.size else 0.0
    if np.isfinite(m) and m > 0.0:
        f = 1.0 / np.sqrt(m) * (1.0 - 1e-10)
        a *= f
        b *= f * f
    else:
        return a

    sum_a = float(np.sum(a))
    old_sum = -1.0
    tiny = 1e-14

    for _ in range(max_passes):
        if time.monotonic() > deadline:
            break
        if old_sum >= 0.0 and (sum_a - old_sum) < 1e-9 * max(1.0, sum_a):
            break
        old_sum = sum_a

        if order == 0:
            idx = np.arange(n)
        elif order == 1:
            idx = np.arange(n - 1, -1, -1)
        else:
            idx = rng.permutation(n)

        count = 0
        time_break = False
        for i in idx:
            count += 1
            if (count & 255) == 0 and time.monotonic() > deadline:
                time_break = True
                break

            x = float(a[i])
            dl = 2 * i

            # Diagonal constraint: independent part + x^2 <= 1.
            cdiag = float(b[dl]) - x * x
            val = 1.0 - cdiag
            if val < 1e-12:
                val = 0.0
            elif val > 1.0:
                val = 1.0
            upper = float(np.sqrt(val))

            off_min = float("inf")

            # Off-diagonal constraints for j < i: lags i .. 2i-1.
            if i > 0:
                la = a[:i]
                if np.any(la > tiny):
                    ls = np.clip(1.0 - b[i:2 * i], 0.0, None)
                    mask = la > tiny
                    if np.any(mask):
                        ratio = float(np.min(ls[mask] / (2.0 * la[mask])))
                        off_min = min(off_min, x + ratio)

            # Off-diagonal constraints for j > i: lags 2i+1 .. i+n-1.
            if i < n - 1:
                ra = a[i + 1:]
                if np.any(ra > tiny):
                    rs = np.clip(1.0 - b[2 * i + 1:i + n], 0.0, None)
                    mask = ra > tiny
                    if np.any(mask):
                        ratio = float(np.min(rs[mask] / (2.0 * ra[mask])))
                        off_min = min(off_min, x + ratio)

            new_x = upper
            if off_min < new_x:
                new_x = off_min
            if new_x < x:
                new_x = x

            d = new_x - x
            if d > 1e-15:
                # Update affected convolution coefficients exactly.
                delta = 2.0 * d * a
                delta[i] += d * d
                b[i:i + n] += delta
                a[i] = new_x
                sum_a += d
            else:
                a[i] = new_x

        if time_break:
            break

        # Correct floating drift conservatively.
        maxb = float(np.max(b)) if b.size else 0.0
        if maxb > 1.0 + 1e-8:
            f = np.sqrt(1.0 / maxb) * (1.0 - 1e-9)
            a *= f
            b *= f * f
            sum_a = float(np.sum(a))

    return a


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400):
    """
    Multi-start search for low normalized linear-convolution peak.

    Strategy:
      1. Use the analytic q_i = binom(2i,i)/4^i truncations as strong baselines.
         These have exact early convolution coefficients equal to 1.
      2. For small lengths, run derivative-free simplex optimizations.
      3. Run many random starts with a monotone coordinate best-response
         algorithm for the cap-1 formulation:
             maximize sum(a) subject to a >= 0, conv(a,a) <= 1.
         This explores asymmetric sequences more aggressively than simple
         multiplicative perturbations of a uniform parent.
      4. Keep and return the best sequence found.
    """
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    total = max(0.0, float(budget_s))
    deadline = t0 + total - 12.0
    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed))

    best_seq = None
    best_score = float("inf")

    # -------------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Baseline: analytic truncations of (1-x)^(-1/2).
    # -------------------------------------------------------------------------
    for n in range(2, 121):
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 300:
            break
        a = make_q(n)
        sc = score_fast(a)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
            best_score = sc
            best_seq = a.copy()
            print(f"[q-baseline] n={n} score={sc:.9f}")

    if best_seq is None:
        best_seq = make_q(9)
        best_score = score_fast(best_seq)

    # -------------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Very small-n global-ish optimization (DE + Nelder-Mead).
    # -------------------------------------------------------------------------
    if HAVE_SCIPY:
        de_deadline = min(deadline, t0 + 170.0)
        print("Starting small-n differential evolution ...")
        for n in [4, 5, 6, 7, 8]:
            if time.monotonic() > de_deadline:
                break
            try:
                res = sp_de(
                    softmax_objective,
                    bounds=[(-7.0, 7.0)] * (n - 1),
                    args=(n,),
                    maxiter=120,
                    popsize=15,
                    seed=int(rng.integers(0, 10_000_000)),
                    tol=1e-9,
                    workers=1,
                )
                if np.isfinite(res.fun):
                    a = normalize_softmax(res.x, n)
                    sc = score_fast(a)
                    if sc < best_score:
                        best_score = sc
                        best_seq = a.copy()
                        print(f"[DE] n={n} score={sc:.9f}")
            except Exception:
                pass

        local_deadline = min(deadline, t0 + 520.0)
        print("Starting small-n local simplex search ...")
        tasks = [
            (7, 14), (8, 18), (9, 18), (10, 16), (11, 14),
            (12, 12), (13, 10), (14, 8), (16, 7), (18, 6)
        ]
        for n, starts in tasks:
            if time.monotonic() > local_deadline:
                break
            bx, bf = run_softmax_search(n, rng, starts, local_deadline)
            if bx is not None and np.isfinite(bf):
                a = normalize_softmax(bx, n)
                sc = score_fast(a)
                if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
                    best_score = sc
                    best_seq = a.copy()
                    print(f"[softmax] n={n} score={sc:.9f}")

    # -------------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Cap-1 coordinate best-response search from many starts.
    # -------------------------------------------------------------------------
    print("Starting cap-1 coordinate search ...")
    cap_tasks = [
        (9, 900), (10, 800), (8, 700), (11, 700), (12, 700),
        (7, 600), (6, 500), (13, 600), (14, 500), (15, 500),
        (16, 400), (18, 350), (20, 350), (24, 300), (32, 250),
        (48, 180), (64, 150), (96, 120), (128, 100), (192, 70),
        (256, 55), (384, 35), (512, 25)
    ]

    for n, max_attempts in cap_tasks:
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 15:
            break

        qn = make_q(n)
        max_passes = 90 if n <= 32 else 50 if n <= 128 else 25

        for att in range(max_attempts):
            if time.monotonic() > deadline - 15:
                break
            if att == 0 and n <= 24:
                # Explicitly exploit q with order variations.
                kind = 0
                slack = float(rng.choice(np.array([0.8, 0.9, 0.95, 0.98, 0.85])))
                order = int(rng.integers(0, 3))
            else:
                kind = int(rng.integers(0, 10))
                slack = float(rng.uniform(0.55, 0.97))
                order = int(rng.integers(0, 3))

            base = make_random_base(n, kind, rng)
            a0 = scale_base_to_slack(base, slack)
            if float(np.sum(a0)) <= 0.0:
                continue

            a = boost_cap1(a0, order, max_passes, deadline - 15, rng)
            sc = score_fast(a)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
                best_score = sc
                best_seq = a.copy()
                if n <= 64 or sc < best_score:  # print informative small-n improvements
                    print(f"[cap1] n={n} kind={kind} slack={slack:.3f} score={sc:.9f}")

    # -------------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Final exact validation and safety fallback.
    # -------------------------------------------------------------------------
    if best_seq is None:
        best_seq = make_q(9)

    # Prefer small sequences for exact validation; large ones are still okay but
    # costly if unexpectedly problematic.
    if best_seq is not None and best_seq.size > 2048:
        # If a huge sequence is the best, also keep it, but create a fallback.
        fallback = make_q(9)
    else:
        fallback = make_q(9)

    candidates = []
    if best_seq is not None:
        candidates.append([float(x) for x in best_seq])
    candidates.append([float(x) for x in fallback])

    # If the current best for unknown numerical reasons is bad, fall back to q9.
    try:
        exact = float(evaluate_sequence(candidates[0]))
    except Exception:
        exact = float("inf")

    if not np.isfinite(exact):
        try:
            exact = float(evaluate_sequence(candidates[1]))
        except Exception:
            exact = float("inf")
        if np.isfinite(exact):
            candidates = candidates[1:]
    else:
        # If best exact score is nevertheless above the guaranteed q9 fallback,
        # switch to fallback only if q9 is better exactly.
        try:
            fb_exact = float(evaluate_sequence(fallback))
        except Exception:
            fb_exact = float("inf")
        if np.isfinite(fb_exact):
            if exact > fb_exact + 1e-12:
                candidates[0] = [float(x) for x in fallback]
                exact = fb_exact
        else:
            candidates.insert(0, [float(x) for x in fallback])

    seq = candidates[0]

    # Final nonnegative sanity pass.
    seq = [max(0.0, float(x)) for x in seq]
    try:
        final_score = float(evaluate_sequence(seq))
        if not np.isfinite(final_score):
            seq = [float(x) for x in fallback]
            final_score = float(evaluate_sequence(seq))
        print(f"Final candidate n={len(seq)} exact_score={final_score:.9f}")
    except Exception:
        # If evaluate_sequence cannot be called for any reason, return a valid
        # deterministic construction.
        seq = [float(x) for x in fallback]

    return seq
```
